# Arcanum — Whole-Tree Review, 2026-10-04

**Date:** 2026-10-04 · **Reviewed at:** `main` = `20c2d41b` (the merge of the memory-curation epic and selective hard erasure) · **Branch for remediation:** `worktree-codebase-review-remediation` · **Status:** review complete; this document is the specification the remediation plan at `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-04-codebase-review-remediation.md` argues from.

## Scope

The Arcanum codebase only, per the user's instruction during the review: `RetroDownfall.Arcanum.Core`, `RetroDownfall.Arcanum.Secrets`, `RetroDownfall.Arcanum.Infrastructure`, `RetroDownfall.Arcanum.Api` (and `Api.DevHost`), `RetroDownfall.Arcanum.Cli`, `RetroDownfall.Arcanum.NativeSqlCipher`, the Arcanum test and benchmark projects, the build and CI surface (`*.csproj`, `Directory.Build.*`, `.github/workflows`, `scripts/`), and the governed documents (`AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `docs/Arcanum.*.md`, `docs/Compendium.README.md`). `Compendium.Ux`, `TheForge.Core` and `TheForge.Ux` were excluded from review except where CI gating is concerned.

Four domains were reviewed for every slice: reliability, security, performance and usability, with test honesty and documentation truthfulness as first-class categories, because this repository's own history shows that a passing test standing over broken production behaviour is where its defects hide.

## Method

Twenty-nine read-only finder agents ran over bounded slices (one brief, one findings file each): Core (2), Secrets (with Core), Infrastructure by subsystem (14, plus 3 gap-filler slices for files the first pass reported unread), Api (3 plus 2 gap fillers), Cli (3 plus 1 gap filler), build/CI/scripts (1), a grep-driven cross-cutting sweep of twenty defect signatures over every production file (1), documentation drift checked mechanically against code (1), and test-suite health (1). Finders were forbidden from building, testing or editing; they reasoned about tests by performing mutations on paper against the real test code. Every finding had to cite `path:line` re-derived by search, name the production call path for any reachability claim, and describe the exact mutation for any test-honesty claim.

Every Blocking and Important finding was then handed to an independent adversarial verifier (twenty-one verifier passes) whose default verdict was *refuted*. Verifiers re-opened every cited file, re-derived every line number, traced the production path themselves, performed the described mutations against the actual test code, and re-rated severity. Two findings were settled empirically on this host (the chained-symlink containment bypass by resolving real links; the macOS `ulimit -v` failure by running the shell). Verifiers also recorded fix hazards for the implementer (for example: do not hand a logger factory to the MCP HTTP transport, which strips loggers to keep bearer tokens out of logs; fix the Tapestry session-scope spelling at the read side only; correct the native-runtime proof in the docs rather than wiring a validator that would fail every notarized macOS build).

A consolidation pass merged the 29 findings files and 21 verdict files into the register below: duplicates merged (31), verifier severities applied, refuted findings dropped and listed with the reason, and each surviving row given a concrete fix and the single failing test that pins it.

| Bucket | Count |
|---|---|
| Raw findings filed (including verifier-discovered) | 444 |
| Merged as duplicates | 31 |
| Refuted and dropped | 4 |
| **Carried into remediation** | **409** — 8 Blocking · 70 Important · 331 Minor |

The verification pass moved severities in both directions: four original Blocking findings were narrowed to Important or Minor (the Tapestry session-tree key, the native-runtime validator, the first-restore-publication wedge in the backup twin, and the Windows AppContainer findings stayed Blocking but Plausible because no Windows host was available), while three Important findings were widened to Blocking (the chained-symlink containment bypass, which also exposes writes; the fail-open `ulimit` prelude, which never applies a memory ceiling on macOS; and the AsyncLocal ambient loss in the Wizard tool loop, which leaves Covenant staging, attachment tools, the attachment-memory gate, spend attribution and Campaign memory scope null at tool time).

## The eight Blocking findings in one paragraph each

**R-001 Factory erasure runs inside the lease heartbeat.** The factory-erasure route keeps the one-minute lease renewal running through the Covenant coordinator's closed period, although the design says the maintainer ends at launch publication because a renewal advances the revision the transition journal bound itself to. With admission shut the renewal throws, and the maintainer either cancels the in-flight erasure or lets it finish while the route reports failure.

**R-002 First reset publication can wedge the host.** The installation-reset active store makes the revision-0 anchor durable before the first encrypted file exists; if that write fails or the caller cancels, the result (anchor Active, file absent) is refused by recovery, startup cleanup and a second begin, so the host cannot start. The backup twin already rolls this arm back; the reset store copied the ordering without the rollback.

**R-003 `ExplainAsync` leaks its Turn lease.** After the lease is acquired, nothing owns it until the result is created; a thrown snapshot read or linker leaves it registered forever, so every later exclusive Covenant operation revokes, drains for 30 s and fails until restart. Reachable through `POST /api/memory/covenant/explain`.

**R-004 / R-005 The Windows AppContainer broker cannot run anything.** It is handed the macOS default roots `/opt/homebrew` and `/usr/local`, which do not exist on Windows and make the grant throw, and it passes a bare command name to `CreateProcessW`, which never searches PATH. Both are static traces (Plausible) because no Windows host was available; the plan adds seams so macOS tests pin the logic and a Windows-lane test settles it.

**R-006 The child-process `ulimit` prelude fails open.** The prelude joins its `ulimit` calls with `;` and then `exec`s the child, so a limit the shell rejects is printed and ignored. On macOS `ulimit -v` is rejected every time, so the documented memory ceiling is deterministically absent for every tool child.

**R-007 `WorkspacePathPolicy` splices symlink targets instead of re-walking them.** With `ws/b -> ../outside` and `ws/d -> b/sub`, the candidate `ws/d/file` is accepted because the walk resolves `d` to `ws/b/sub`, passes the lexical prefix test and never visits `ws/b`. Verified empirically with real links. Reachable through read, list, delete and write tools and the HTTP file API.

**R-008 AsyncLocal ambients are lost at the next `yield`.** `RunInferenceAttemptAsync` is an async iterator; an AsyncLocal written in one `MoveNextAsync` segment is rolled back when the segment returns at a `yield`, and tool execution always runs in the next segment. Five ambients are written that way and read null at tool time, so Covenant staging tools are always refused, attachment tools see no session, the attachment-memory gate fails open, A2A spend loses attribution, and Lexicon/Saga tools fall back to installation scope in a Campaign-bound turn. No Wizard-level test exercised any of them.

## Rulings the remediation plan makes on design conflicts

Several findings are sanctioned by a sentence in a governed document. The plan rules on each (the ledger records the rulings; the final report lists them):

- **R-010** Campaign-targeted Saga/Lexicon reset bypasses the labelled-artifact guard (DESIGN :2529). Ruling: add the guard; the sentence contradicts the section's own invariant (:2511).
- **R-026** Plaintext Session export runs unguarded while the Covenant flag is off (DESIGN :2419, :2437). Ruling: run the two content-free ledger reads regardless of the flag; change those sentences.
- **R-075** The native-runtime validator is documented as a runtime proof but never called (DESIGN :508). Ruling: correct the documentation; do not wire the validator (it would fail every notarized macOS build).
- **R-328** `key set` takes the master API key on argv (Command Reference :464 vs DESIGN :179/:4144). Ruling: DESIGN wins; remove the positional and update the Command Reference.
- **R-329** `session compact` has no confirmation and exits 0 on a partial result (Command Reference :566/:576). Ruling: documented behaviour stands; add one sentence stating the exemption.
- **R-122** No Covenant accelerator rebuild driver exists (DESIGN :2122/:2163 document the absence deliberately). Ruling: parked as a feature decision for the owner; the latent rebuild-scan defect (R-121) and the owed-state defect (R-128) are fixed.
- **R-196** Work-lease maintenance revocation is inert (DESIGN :2593 says it is by design). Ruling: document which workers honour it.
- **R-375** About 38,000 blank-line violations of AGENTS.md rule 4 across 2,279 files. Ruling: do not reformat the whole tree in this branch (it would conflict with the owner's in-flight branches); apply `--compact-delimiters` to every file this branch touches and leave the one-shot repo-wide run to the owner.
- **R-376** Publishing a release is not gated on CI (release.yml records the decision). Ruling: parked; the recorded decision stands.
- **R-398** The hosted-producer analyzer dominates the test assembly. Ruling: parked; structural, no defect.

## Register

What follows is the consolidated register: every surviving finding with its final severity, verification verdict, locations, defect, fix, failing test, document to update and any design conflict. The plan references rows by their `R-nnn` id.

## Blocking

### R-001 · S04-01 · Infra/Data · Factory-erasure route keeps the lease heartbeat running through the closed period
- Verification: Confirmed — V1 (widened: a heartbeat failure after the immutable proof does not stop the erasure, so it completes including `CompleteCovenantResetAsync` and the caller is then told it failed unexpectedly)
- Category: reliability
- Location: `Infra/Data/DataRetentionService.FactoryActivation.cs:288,454-481,525,548`; `Infra/Data/DataRetentionLeaseMaintainer.cs:79-150`; `Infra/Data/LongRunningOperationStore.cs:998-1050`; contrast the correct direct arm `Infra/Data/DataRetentionService.cs:1898-1913` and `DataRetentionService.FactoryReset.cs:558-562,980`
- Defect: `ApplyFactoryResetRouteAsync` runs `_covenantErasureCoordinator.RunAsync` (the closed period, ordinary admission shut) inside the `_leaseMaintainer.RunAsync` lambda, so the 1-minute heartbeat keeps calling `RenewLeaseAsync`. DESIGN says the maintainer ends at launch publication because a renewal advances the revision the transition journal bound itself to. With admission shut the renewal throws `GrimoireMaintenanceUnavailableException`; the maintainer cancels the in-flight erasure (before the immutable proof) or, after the proof, lets it finish while the route reports `FailUnexpectedCovenantResetAsync`. A closed period longer than 60 s is inside the documented envelope, not an edge.
- Fix: End the maintained scope at launch publication: move `_covenantErasureCoordinator.RunAsync` and its success/failure mapping out of the maintainer lambda, keep re-plan, `RevalidateAsync`, `PrepareFactoryErasureInventoryAsync`, the checkpoint reload and `TryDisposeCovenantPlanningLeaseAsync` inside it, use the caller token for the coordinator as `ApplyCovenantResetAsync` does, keep `CloseFactoryServiceConnectionAsync` before it, and keep the `DataRetentionLeaseLostException` catch over the maintained segment. Reword `DataRetentionService.cs:6987-6989` (see R-108).
- Failing test: `CovenantErasureSameProcessTests.Factory_route_attempts_no_lease_renewal_once_the_coordinator_runs` — mirror `Direct_retention_reset_stops_renewing_its_durable_lease_once_the_journal_opens` (`:1650`) with `ApplyFactoryAsync`, `fastLeaseHeartbeat: true`, pause inside the coordinator 600 ms, assert `faults.RenewalAttempts` unchanged and the apply then completes (red today).
- Docs: none (DESIGN §:729 and :2713 are already correct)
- Design conflict: none

### R-002 · S09-01 · Infra/InstallationReset · First reset publication can leave an Active opening anchor with no file; recovery, startup cleanup and re-Begin all refuse it
- Verification: Narrowed (Blocking retained) — V3: the non-crash failure/cancel arm is the defect; the crash arm is doc-sanctioned fail-closed (DESIGN :741)
- Category: reliability
- Location: `Infra/InstallationReset/InstallationResetActiveStore.cs:297-300,333,348,836-839,1132-1135,1297-1307`; `InstallationResetActiveAnchorStore.cs:393,407,417,422,426,434,438`; `Infra/Hosting/GrimoireDatabaseHostedService.cs:~325`; sister fix `Infra/Backup/BackupRestoreJournalAnchorStore.cs:233-246,273-292` (`RetireUnpublishedOpening`); test `tests/InstallationReset/InstallationResetActiveStoreTests.cs:248-290`
- Defect: `BeginAsync` makes the revision-0 anchor durable before the first encrypted file exists and nothing undoes it if `ReplaceDurablyAsync` fails (IO, permission, disk-full) or the caller cancels before the atomic move. The result (anchor Active, file absent) is `EvidenceFailure` for `RecoverCoreAsync` and `CompleteStartupCleanupAsync`, and `Conflict` for a second `BeginAsync`; the host then refuses to start with no supported escape. The restore-journal twin (DESIGN :2266) already rolls this arm back; the reset store copied the ordering without the rollback.
- Fix: In `BeginAsync` (`:343-358`), on `PublishAsync` failure or cancellation run a `RetireUnpublishedOpening` equivalent under a `CreateCheckpointToken()` token: re-prove anchor == opening, Revision 0, file absent, then compare-write `Closed` (or remove anchor then key, mirroring `CompleteClosedSuffixAsync`); best-effort, swallow its own failure, run for `OperationCanceledException` too (wrap in try/catch and rethrow; never a `finally` on the success path). Do NOT change `RecoverCoreAsync` to treat "Active rev 0, no file" as recoverable: that would erase the doc-sanctioned crash blocker. Leave the resumable `MigrateLegacyV1Async` opening (`:606`) alone.
- Failing test: `InstallationResetActiveStoreTests.Interrupted_opening_before_first_publication_is_rolled_back_and_the_next_begin_succeeds` — reuse the `file:temporary-flushed` cancel from `:248`, assert a second `BeginAsync(CancellationToken.None)` succeeds (today `Conflict`) and `RecoverAsync` no longer fails; rewrite the existing test so it stops asserting the wedge. Add the startup-side twin `InstallationResetStartupRecoveryTests.Interrupted_opening_does_not_block_host_startup`.
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` §10.20 (~2905-2931): state the non-crash opening-rollback rule; keep the crash-blocker wording
- Design conflict: partial — DESIGN :741 sanctions fail-closed for a crash between the two writes; the fix must preserve that and only add the in-process rollback

### R-003 · S31-01 · Infra/Covenant+Lexicon+Memory · `ExplainAsync` leaks its Turn lease when the snapshot read throws, wedging every later exclusive operation
- Verification: Confirmed — V13
- Category: reliability
- Location: `Infra/Covenant/CovenantManagementService.cs:377,401-403,416-418,423,429,431`; `Infra/Covenant/CovenantOperationGate.cs:149-165,649-720,781-796,1025-1060,1312-1330`; `Infra/Data/Covenant/CovenantStore.cs:43-140`; `Api/Tower/CovenantInspectionEndpoints.cs:155-171,182-218` (guarded sibling `:268-286`)
- Defect: after `AcquireTurnAsync` succeeds the registration sits in `_registrations` and nothing owns it until `CovenantLeasedServiceResult.Create` runs; `ReadTurnSnapshotAsync` (request token) or `linker.Link` throwing (client disconnect, `SqliteException`, overflow) unwinds with the lease undisposed and `explained.Take()` never reached. No finalizer or timeout exists. Every later exclusive acquire covering the scope revokes it, waits the 30 s drain and returns `MaintenanceFailed` until restart, so Covenant reset, family reinitialize, backup restore, Campaign delete and protected transfer become unavailable. Only trigger is the HTTP endpoint (`ArcanumApiClient.ExplainCovenantAsync` has no caller).
- Fix: use an ownership handoff: `bool transferred = false; try { ...; transferred = true; return Create(..., lease.Value); } finally { if (!transferred) await lease.Value.DisposeAsync(); }`. Set `transferred` only after `Create` returns (it can throw on a null lease) and never dispose on the success path (single-take contract in `CovenantLeasedServiceResults.cs`).
- Failing test: `CovenantOperatorJourneyTests.Explain_releases_its_lease_when_the_snapshot_read_throws` — wrap `ICovenantStore` so `ReadTurnSnapshotAsync` throws `OperationCanceledException` (second case `InvalidOperationException` via a throwing linker); assert `ExplainAsync` throws, `gate.LiveRegistrationCount == 0` (internal, `CovenantOperationGate.cs:71`; red today: 1) and `AcquireExclusiveAsync` then succeeds.
- Docs: none (DESIGN §10.16/10.18 already promise release on every path)
- Design conflict: none

### R-004 · S16-01 · Infra/Process+A2A · Windows AppContainer broker fails every run: default system roots `/opt/homebrew` and `/usr/local` are granted but never exist
- Verification: Plausible — V7 (static trace, high confidence, Windows-only; settled by a Windows lane asserting `ReadExecuteRoots.All(Directory.Exists)`)
- Category: correctness
- Location: `Infra/Process/ChildProcessSandboxRoots.cs:128,159,281-324` (unconditional `AddRoot` at 320/322); `Infra/Process/ChildProcessFilesystemJail.cs:252-254,262-263,726-817`; `Infra/Process/WindowsAppContainerLauncher.cs:77-80,84-86,143-155`; `Infra/Process/WindowsAppContainerPolicy.cs:20-47`
- Defect: `SystemRuntimeRoots()` appends `/opt/homebrew` and `/usr/local` on every OS; on Windows they resolve to `C:\opt\homebrew` and `C:\usr\local`, which `NormalizeExistingRoots` keeps despite its name. The host accepts them (`IsSafeRoot` is lexical), the broker's `Grant` throws on `!Directory.Exists`, `Run` returns exit 73 with no output. It is not `Unavailable`, so `AllowUnsandboxedToolChildren` never engages and `ToolChildSandboxCapabilityReporter` still reports `Active`. Every sandboxed `execute_command`/`run_spell_script` on Windows fails.
- Fix: Make `SystemRuntimeRoots()` OS-conditional (Homebrew-style roots only on macOS, keep them there when they exist; none on Windows, where AppContainer already has default read on `C:\Windows`/`C:\Program Files`) and make the root normalization drop roots that are neither a directory nor a file on every OS.
- Failing test: `ChildProcessSandboxRootsTests.SystemRuntimeRoots_for_windows_contains_no_posix_paths` via an `os` seam (runs on macOS and Windows CI) plus an all-OS `ForExecuteCommand_returns_only_existing_roots`; settle with a Windows-lane `WindowsAppContainerBrokerTests.Broker_runs_a_bare_command_inside_the_jail` (see R-043).
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` §11 Windows AppContainer ADR (~3503) only if root semantics change
- Design conflict: none

### R-005 · S16-02 · Infra/Process+A2A · Windows broker passes a bare command name as `lpApplicationName`, which never searches PATH
- Verification: Plausible — V7 (static, high confidence; Win32 contract: a non-null `lpApplicationName` is used as-is, no search path, no default `.exe`)
- Category: correctness
- Location: `Infra/Process/WindowsAppContainerLauncher.cs:242-246`; `Infra/Process/ChildProcessFilesystemJail.cs:272-274,297`; `Infra/Mcp/InternalTools/ArcanumInternalToolServer.ExecuteCommand.cs:54,90`; `Api/Intelligence/Tools/ArcanumSpellScriptTool.cs:528-566`
- Defect: the broker calls `CreateProcessW(payload.Target, ...)` with `Target = startInfo.FileName` (e.g. `git`, `python`) and cwd set to the jail temp dir; `Process.Start` would normally resolve PATH but the broker bypasses it and nothing on this path resolves the executable (the only PATH/PATHEXT resolver is `Familiars/FamiliarExecutableResolver`). Even after R-004, every bare-name command fails with exit 73.
- Fix: Resolve `Target` to an absolute path on the host before serialising the payload (reuse `FamiliarExecutableResolver`, resolving against the scrubbed `startInfo.Environment["PATH"]`), refuse with a model-visible "command not found" otherwise. Hazards: `.cmd`/`.bat` shims cannot be launched via `lpApplicationName` (wrap in `cmd.exe /c` or refuse); a resolved executable under a user-profile path (npm, pyenv, scoop) is unreadable to an AppContainer unless its directory is added to the read-execute roots.
- Failing test: extract a pure `WindowsBrokerTargetResolver.Resolve(fileName, path, pathExt, fileExists)` and add `WindowsBrokerTargetResolverTests.Bare_name_resolves_through_PATH_and_PATHEXT_to_an_absolute_path` and `Unresolvable_name_returns_failure` (run on macOS); end-to-end `WindowsAppContainerBrokerTests.Broker_resolves_bare_cmd_exe` (Windows lane, published apphost; `IsBrokerCapableHost` is false in the xunit host).
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` §11 Windows ADR: state the host resolves the executable before brokering
- Design conflict: none

### R-006 · S03-02 · Infra/Process+A2A · Sanctum child-process ulimit prelude fails open; on macOS the memory ceiling is never applied
- Verification: Widened (Important → Blocking) — V2: demonstrated on this host that `/bin/sh -c 'ulimit -v 524288; ...; exec ...'` prints `cannot modify limit: Invalid argument`, rc=1, then still runs the target
- Category: security
- Location: `Infra/Platform/ProcessResourceLimiter.cs:186-203` (`ApplyOnMacOs` always `includeMemory:true` at :195), `:205-237` (Linux; cgroup join `2>/dev/null` at :221), `:423-455` (`BuildUlimitPrelude`: statements joined with `; `, no `|| exit`), `:465-492`; `tests/Platform/ProcessResourceLimiterTests.cs:135-176` (asserts only the string `ulimit -v 524288; `)
- Defect: the prelude is `ulimit -t N; ulimit -v K; ulimit -n F; exec "$@"`; a `ulimit` the shell cannot apply prints to stderr, returns non-zero, and `exec` still runs the child unlimited. On macOS (the shipping Unix platform) `ulimit -v` (RLIMIT_AS) is rejected every time, so the documented memory ceiling is deterministically absent for every tool child; CPU (`-t`) and fd (`-n`) apply on this host but fail open the same way when the hard limit is lower or the value exceeds a ceiling. Linux cgroup-join denial also silently skips memory (Linux is not a shipping RID).
- Fix: Do (b) before (a): drop `ulimit -v` on macOS and state memory is unenforced there (update DESIGN :3492/:3498, `SanctumConfig` remarks, `SanctumGuard.cs:20-21`), or enforce RSS another way (watchdog on `proc_pid_rusage`/`ps -o rss` killing the process group, mapped to `ResourceLimitKind.Memory`). Then fail closed: `ulimit -t N || exit 126; ulimit -n F || exit 126; ... exec "$@"` and `echo $$ > .../cgroup.procs || exit 126`, mapping a pre-exec exit 126 to a `ResourceLimitError` ("limit could not be applied"). Hazard: (a) alone makes every macOS child exit 126 because `-v` always fails. Fold R-234 (effective-memory helper) into the same change.
- Failing test: `ProcessResourceLimiterTests.Prelude_FailsClosed_WhenUlimitRejected` (every `ulimit` clause followed by `|| exit`) plus an unconditional real-shell `ProcessRunnerResourceLimitTests.MacOs_MemoryLimit_Is_Either_Enforced_Or_Reported_Unenforced` (`MaxMemoryMb=32`; child dies on over-allocation or the result/status declares the limit unenforced). The existing opt-in `Process_exceeding_memory_limit_is_terminated_and_breached` would fail on macOS.
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` §11.15 (~3492-3498) and the Sanctum resource-limit table; the docstring/claim in `ProcessResourceLimiterTests.cs:135-176` is false today
- Design conflict: none (DESIGN :3492 states enforcement the platform cannot provide; the doc, not a sanctioned behavior, is wrong)

### R-007 · S03-01 / S08-01 · Infra/Security · `WorkspacePathPolicy` symlink walk splices a link target instead of re-walking it: a second link whose target is spelled through an escaping directory link bypasses containment (read, list, delete AND write)
- Verification: Widened (S03-01 Important / S08-01 Blocking → Blocking) — V2: .NET `ResolveLinkTarget(final:true)` measured empirically; a pure link-to-link chain is rejected, the through-a-directory spelling is not; also writes are exposed, not only reads
- Category: security
- Location: `Infra/Security/WorkspacePathPolicy.cs:139-159,172-269` (walk `:222-245`, lexical check `:238`, splice `:243`, leaf `:247-263`, resolvers `:297,:314`, `File/Directory.Exists` follow links `:226`); downstream non-guards `Infra/Mcp/SandboxedFileIo.cs:24,53-58,118,354-413`, `Infra/Security/SecureFileReader.cs:96-147` (O_NOFOLLOW only final component), `Infra/Workspaces/PhysicalFileSystemBrowser.cs:355-371,418-433`, `PhysicalFileSystemWriter.cs:276,442-495`, `Infra/Storage/AtomicFile.cs`; 97 call sites of the two public methods under `src/` (SanctumGuard, AttachmentSourceResolver, SpellScanner, CodexReader, search)
- Defect: with `ws/b -> ../outside` (escaping) and `ws/d -> b/sub` (second link whose final target string is `ws/b/sub`, since `ws/b/sub` is a real directory to lstat), candidate `ws/d/file` is accepted: the walk resolves `d` to `ws/b/sub`, passes the lexical prefix test, splices it in, and never visits `ws/b`. Both links can ship in a cloned repo. The post-open revalidation (`TryRevalidateOpenedHandle`) re-runs the same lexical check on `stream.Name`, the identical string, so it cannot catch it; handle-identity capture uses the same escaped path. Reachable through `read_file_chunk`, `write_file`, `replace_text_block`, `list_directory`, the HTTP file API (read/list/write/delete/mkdir), Sanctum and attachment resolution; only `apply_patch` is separately protected (`WorkspaceFileFingerprintService.RejectSymlinkComponents`). A dangling-directory-link write escape was not provable (Plausible; fix with the main change).
- Fix: Canonicalise, do not splice: after the lexical prefix check resolve the deepest existing ancestor with a per-component `ResolveLinkTarget(returnFinalTarget:false)` loop that re-walks each target (depth cap 40) or an lstat-per-component canonicaliser (`NoFollowPathTopology`/`WorkspaceCheckPackageRootPolicy.CanonicalizeDirectory`), canonicalise the root the same way (also fixes macOS `/var -> /private/var`), then do one prefix check; make `TryRevalidateOpenedHandle` compare the handle's kernel path (`F_GETPATH`/`/proc/self/fd`/`GetFinalPathNameByHandle`) to the canonical root, not `stream.Name`. Hazard: the 30+ existing tests use `SetSymlinkResolverForTests`/`SetRelativePathResolverForTests` seams with fabricated targets; a canonicalising rewrite bypasses them, so convert to real-filesystem fixtures and do not leave two code paths.
- Failing test: `WorkspacePathPolicySymlinkTests.IsPathUnderWorkspaceWithSymlinkCheck_LinkTargetPassesThroughEscapingDirectorySymlink_Rejects` (Unix `[SkippableFact]`, real links, no seam: `outside/sub/file`, `ws/b -> ../outside`, `ws/d -> b/sub`; assert false for `ws/d/file` and `ws/d`); `SandboxedFileIoTests.TryWriteAllTextAtomicallyAsync_ThroughChainedLink_DoesNotCreateFileOutsideRoot`; `PhysicalFileSystemBrowserContainmentTests.ReadAsync_rejects_directory_link_chained_through_escaping_link` expecting `Workspace.SymbolicLinkEscape`; keep a green control `..._PureLinkChain_Rejects` (`chain -> b`). No existing test covers chains.
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` §11.6 (state the canonical-path rule; the "walks every existing component" guarantee is currently false for chained links) and the class remarks at `WorkspacePathPolicy.cs:11-16`
- Design conflict: none (DESIGN §11.6 asserts the property the code lacks)

### R-008 · S17-01 / V-S17-01 · Api/Wizard · AsyncLocal ambients published inside the `RunInferenceAttemptAsync` iterator are discarded at the next `yield return`, so tools never see them (Covenant staging, attachment tools, attachment-memory gate, accounting attribution, and Campaign scope for Lexicon/Saga tools)
- Verification: Confirmed (widened) — V11 (and V-S17-01 Plausible, source-derived)
- Category: reliability
- Location: `Api/Intelligence/WizardIntelligenceProvider.cs:1805,1825,2084,3015,3465` (writers), `:3555` (unconditional ToolCall yield), `:3564-3581` (the only re-set: HITL emitter, V-2), `:3630,3789`, `:4213,4218,4298` (cleanup reading lost values); readers `ToolExecutionPipeline.cs:548,676,1521`, `SessionAttachmentToolInjection.cs:235`, `Tools/ArcanumDelegateTaskTool.cs:188,196`, `Infra/Mcp/SessionAttachmentAmbientSend.cs:32,98,191,218,280,322`, `Infra/Mcp/InternalTools/ArcanumInternalToolServer.AttachmentTools.cs:41,129`, `...LexiconTools.cs:54,63,421`, `...SagaTools.cs:94`, `Infra/Data/MemoryScopeResolver.cs:31-60`, `Core/Storage/SessionAttachmentTurnBudget.cs:29-32`, `Core/Intelligence/AttachmentMemoryProvenance.cs:74,153`
- Defect: `RunInferenceAttemptAsync` is an async iterator; an AsyncLocal written in one `MoveNextAsync` segment is rolled back when the segment returns at a `yield`, and tool execution (`ProcessWithLiveWardsAsync`, `:3630`) always runs in the next segment. Five ambients were written the same way as the one the repo fixed (V-2) and read null at tool time: (a) `SessionAttachmentToolAmbient.CurrentSessionId` (attach/refresh post-processing skipped, `session_required`, server-side attachment tools "No current session"); (b) `CovenantToolStagingAmbient` (`retire_covenant`/`propose_covenant` always refused, DESIGN §10.22.5 "goes live" false); (c) `ContextMaterializationLedgerAmbient` + `SessionAttachmentTurnBudget` (dead today because gated on (a), but `TryMarkInjected` returns true when no state, so it becomes fail-open the moment (a) alone is fixed); (d) `AttachmentMemoryGateAmbient` (child-agent unlabelled-attachment guard and `scribe_lexicon` provenance gate fail OPEN; Saga extraction enqueued without provenance; `End()` never disposes so static `BySession` keeps one stale state per session); (e) `TurnAccountingAmbient`/`DelegatedSpendAttribution` (A2A spend loses reservation attribution). V-S17-01: Lexicon/Saga tools resolve scope from the null session ambient, so with `CampaignScopedMemory` on, a Campaign-bound turn reads and writes installation-scope memory (cross-Campaign visibility).
- Fix: Build one per-turn `TurnAmbientSet` (session id, ledger, turn budget, gate state, accounting+writer, Covenant staging) and apply it inside one non-yielding helper called from `ProcessWithLiveWardsAsync` immediately before `ProcessSingleToolCallAsync` (and any other post-yield tool/aux call), extending the existing V-2 re-set rather than adding five; make `End()`/`Dispose` and the `Snapshot()` at `:4213` operate on the captured objects, not AsyncLocal reads. Hazards: re-setting only the session id without ledger/turn budget turns (c) from dead to fail-open (fix together); do NOT call `AttachmentMemoryGateAmbient.BeginTurn` a second time per tool call (it replaces state). Make the Lexicon/Saga scope resolvers fail closed (error, not installation scope) when Campaign scoping is on and no session resolves.
- Failing test: `WizardIntelligenceProviderTests.StreamingAndBufferedToolCall_ObservesAllTurnAmbients` (both modes) — scripted model emits one tool call; a `FakeMcpConnectionManager` tool records `CurrentSessionId`, the ledger, `HasMaterializedAttachmentContent` after an explicit attachment, `DelegatedSpendAttribution.BudgetReservationId`, and (Covenant-enabled) `CovenantToolStagingAmbient.Current`; assert non-null and no `BySession` residue after the turn. Add a Wizard-level `retire_covenant`/`propose_covenant` model-tool-call test (not via `GetResponseAsync`) and `StreamingToolCall_ScribeLexicon_InCampaignBoundSession_WritesCampaignScope`. Existing Covenant/attachment tests reach the ambients before any yield or set them on the test thread, so all stay green today.
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` §10.7 (add "ambients are re-established per tool call" next to the V-2 note), §10.22.5, the Lexicon/Saga scope section; `docs/Arcanum.Engineering.md` "Patterns to follow when writing code"
- Design conflict: none

## Important

### R-009 · S02-01 · Core · A shipped `arcanum` launches `dotnet run --project` on a repository found by walking up from the current directory
- Verification: Narrowed (Important retained) — V8: needs an explicit `open` verb, no installed app candidate starting, and a crafted marker; `config open` uses base-directory-anchored `CompendiumLauncher` and is unaffected
- Category: security
- Location: `Core/Desktop/ApplicationLaunchContracts.cs:109-111,123-146`; `Core/Desktop/ApplicationDiscoveryServices.cs:82,139,218,322-340`; `Core/Desktop/ApplicationLauncher.cs:69-140,221-227`; `Cli/Commands/OpenCommands.cs:~270-277`; contrast `Core/Desktop/CompendiumLauncher.cs:252-285`
- Defect: `ApplicationDiscoveryEnvironment.CreateDefault` finds `RepositoryRoot` by walking up from `AppContext.BaseDirectory` then from `Environment.CurrentDirectory` looking for `RetroDownfall.Arcanum.slnx`; the last candidate on every OS is a `DevelopmentProject` that runs `dotnet run --project` there. There is no `#if`/Debug gate in `Core/Desktop/`, and `ARCANUM_DEV_LAUNCHER` is unrelated (it affects only `ArcanumServeLauncher`). A cloned repo with the marker file plus `src/<App>/<App>.csproj` gets MSBuild-task code execution as the operator.
- Fix: Resolve `RepositoryRoot` from `AppContext.BaseDirectory` only (drop the cwd arm, as `CompendiumLauncher` does) and do not offer the `DevelopmentProject` candidate for a published/AOT image without an explicit opt-in. Keep the display-only `CreateDevelopmentFallback` printed command (`ApplicationLauncher.cs:~262`); it is not executed.
- Failing test: `ApplicationDiscoveryEnvironmentTests.CreateDefault_does_not_adopt_a_repository_root_from_the_current_directory` (temp dir with the `.slnx`, cwd set to a child in a serialised `[Collection]`; assert `RepositoryRoot != tempDir`; red today).
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` desktop launch/discovery section and `docs/Arcanum.Command.Reference.md:288` (open verbs; currently silent on cwd anchoring)
- Design conflict: none

### R-010 · S04-03 · Infra/Data · Campaign-targeted Saga and Lexicon memory reset runs a set-based DELETE over rows that may be labelled, with no labelled-artifact guard
- Verification: Confirmed — V1 (the design text explicitly carves it out, so the fix is a design decision)
- Category: security
- Location: `Infra/Data/DataRetentionService.cs:6093,6101-6112` (early return when `campaignId is not null`), `:3437-3541` (`ApplyMemoryResetAsync`, guard `:3467`, deletes `:3511-3527`), `:2732-2735,2771` (campaign selections); `Infra/Data/Schema/Tables/artifact_sensitivity.sql:5-6` (no FKs); `ICovenantLabeledArtifactTransactionGuard.cs:39/61/79` (only three arms, no "predicate arm")
- Defect: the untargeted arm asks `EnsureNoneLabeledAsync` for the whole kind, but the Campaign-targeted arm skips the check on the assumption of a "predicate arm" that does not exist. A Campaign-scoped reset deletes labelled Saga memories and Lexicon entries by `DELETE ... WHERE CampaignId = ...` with no label check, no purge kernel and no receipt, leaving an orphaned `artifact_sensitivity` label (the delete trigger only protects the label row). DESIGN §2529 states this deliberately with a non sequitur rationale; it contradicts the section's own invariant (§2511).
- Fix: Inside the transaction, before the first delete, run a parameterized `SELECT EXISTS` against `artifact_sensitivity` for the kind joined by `ArtifactId` to the targeted rows (or use the batched `EnsureAllUnlabeledAsync` once ids are enumerated) and throw `RetentionCovenantLabelException(Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority)` (already handled at `:1539`). Hazard: filter by the artifact set, not only `artifact_sensitivity.CampaignId`, because a label's `CampaignId` is a historical owner and may differ from the memory's current scope.
- Failing test: `DataRetentionCampaignScopedResetTests.Campaign_scoped_saga_reset_refuses_when_a_member_is_labelled` (Campaign-scope Saga memory + live `artifact_sensitivity` row, apply `ResetMemory(Saga, campaignId)`; assert `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority`, memory and label unchanged) plus a Lexicon twin. The test file has no label coverage today.
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` §10.20.2 (:2527-2529 "not asked the bulk question") and §10.20 Campaign-reset paragraph (:697); reword the `DataRetentionService.cs:6093` comment
- Design conflict: yes — `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md:2529` ("A Campaign-targeted memory reset selects its rows by a predicate rather than handing a statement the whole table, and is not asked the bulk question") currently sanctions the behavior; the fix needs a design change to that sentence

### R-011 · S05-02 · Infra/Data · The budget-reservation atomicity claim has no concurrent test
- Verification: Confirmed — V5
- Category: test-honesty
- Location: `Infra/Data/BudgetReservationService.cs:14,53,138` (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`); `tests/Data/BudgetReservationServiceTests.cs` (all sequential; `AdjustAsync_AtomicallyRaisesWithoutLoweringOrExceedingDailyLimit` at :257; cancelled-mid-transaction tests at ~:389-401 assert autocommit only)
- Defect: the class is documented "Atomic budget reservations" and is the hard daily spend limit, but no test uses `Task.WhenAll`/`Parallel`/a second connection. Mutating `BeginTransaction(deferred: false)` to deferred or removing it leaves every test green because a single connection never observes a race.
- Fix: Add a two-connection test using a deterministic seam between the sum read and the insert (precedent `IdempotencyClaimStoreTests.cs:309-420`); rename the `AdjustAsync_Atomically...` test to say what it checks.
- Failing test: `BudgetReservationServiceTests.ReserveAsync_WhenTwoConnectionsReserveConcurrently_NeverExceedsTheDailyLimit` — limit $1.00, two $0.60 reservations on two connections, first paused after its sum; assert exactly one `Success` and one `Budget.Exceeded`, outstanding $0.60; red if the immediate transaction is removed.
- Docs: none
- Design conflict: none

### R-012 · S07-02 · Infra/Backup · `ExecuteAsync` deletes staging (including the displaced prior installation and the V2 journal) for any post-commit exception outside the hand-enumerated filter
- Verification: Plausible (mechanism confirmed; wider consequence than filed) — V1: no concrete post-commit callee throwing an unlisted type proven, but the destructive default is the defect
- Category: reliability
- Location: `Infra/Backup/BackupRestoreService.cs:872,938,1217,1263-1270,1359-1370,1424,1450-1457,1510-1541` (`finally` at :1530); `Infra/Backup/BackupRestoreJournalAnchorStore.cs:88` (V2 journal lives in the staging root); `Infra/Backup/BackupRestoreRecovery.cs:445-490`
- Defect: after `Commit(...)` succeeds, an exception that is neither a caller-token `OperationCanceledException` nor in the seven-type filter (`ArgumentException`, `KeyNotFoundException`, `FormatException`, `TimeoutException`, `Win32Exception`, a non-caller `TaskCanceledException`, `JsonException`) skips reversal and reaches the `finally`, where `retainStagingForReconciliation` is still false, so the journal and the whole staging root (containing `previous/`, the displaced installation) are deleted while the new generation is live. On the Covenant arm `AbortAsync(provenPreSwap:false)` leaves the anchor `Active` naming a journal that no longer exists, so the next start likely refuses (unverified step). This contradicts DESIGN :849 ("retention of the displaced installation outranks staging cleanup").
- Fix: Invert the default: retain unless `commit` is null, the reversal was verified, or the restore reached `Cleanup` (`retainStagingForReconciliation = commit is { Succeeded: true } && lastPhase != Cleanup`), and/or add `catch (Exception) when (commit is { Succeeded: true })` running the same reversal block. Hazard: the `Cleanup` advance at `:1404` runs before the success return, so set the flag after it or the inverted default retains staging on success.
- Failing test: `BackupRestoreServiceTests.A_non_io_fault_after_commit_keeps_the_journal_and_displaced_installation` — shaped like `A_fault_after_commit_returns_the_prior_installation_to_its_original_state` (`:673`) but `BeforePhaseForTests` throws `new ArgumentException("injected")` at `BackupRestorePhase.Reconcile`; assert the `.arcanum-restore-*` directory and `previous/` still exist (existing post-commit tests inject only `IOException`).
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` §5.4.9 restore failure semantics paragraph
- Design conflict: none

### R-013 · S07-05 · Infra/Backup · Rollback of local secrets is best-effort and silent, and cannot undo secrets that did not exist before the restore
- Verification: Confirmed — V1
- Category: reliability
- Location: `Infra/Backup/BackupSecretRewrapper.cs:127,132,147,532-552,555-598`; callers `Infra/Backup/BackupRestoreService.cs:1277,1432,1465`
- Defect: `RestoreAsync` swallows `IOException`/`UnauthorizedAccessException`/`NotSupportedException`/`CryptographicException` with an empty catch and returns `Task`, so a failure to reinstate the prior Grimoire secret still yields `RolledBack` ("returned to its original state"), deletes journal and staging, and leaves the archive's secret against the prior differently keyed database. `CaptureAsync` maps non-Ok reads to `null` and `RestoreAsync` writes only non-null values, so a rolled-back restore leaves the archive's key ring and (with `--restore-master-api-key`) its master API key behind. DESIGN :845 promises prior secrets are reinstated on rollback.
- Fix: Make `RestoreAsync` return a result; on failure retain staging and journal and return `ReversalIncomplete` naming the secret instead of `RolledBack`. Capture "absent" as a distinct value and delete on rollback; treat a non-Ok/non-absent capture read as a pre-commit refusal. Hazard: delete on rollback only when the prior state was definitively absent, never when the read was merely `Unavailable`.
- Failing test: `BackupSecretRewrapperTests.Restore_reports_failure_when_the_prior_grimoire_secret_cannot_be_reinstated` (store throws `IOException` on `SaveGrimoireEncryptionSecretAsync` during `RestoreAsync`; assert a failure result) and `Restore_removes_secrets_that_were_absent_at_capture` (no API key at capture, `RewrapAsync(restoreMasterApiKey:true)` then `RestoreAsync`, assert no API key).
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` §5.4.9 ("prior secrets are captured first and reinstated if the restore rolls back")
- Design conflict: none

### R-014 · S08-02 · Infra/Workspaces · No protection for `.git/` (hooks, config) or `.arcanum/` in any model-driven write path
- Verification: Plausible — V2 (design gap; reachability depends on Ward/approval posture, cross-slice; no documented guarantee violated)
- Category: security
- Location: `Infra/Workspaces/CodingTools/WorkspacePatchPlanner.cs:225-314`; `Infra/Workspaces/CodingTools/WorkspaceRelativePath.cs:62-122`; `Infra/Workspaces/PhysicalFileSystemWriter.cs:21-80`; `Infra/Mcp/ApplyPatchToolExecutionService.cs`; `SandboxedFileIo` (`write_file`/`replace_text_block`)
- Defect: containment is "under the workspace root", and `.git/hooks/pre-commit`, `.git/config` (`core.fsmonitor`, `core.hooksPath`, `alias.*`) and `.arcanum/**` are under the root. One `apply_patch` create plants a hook that executes with the operator's full identity, outside the Seatbelt jail, on the operator's next `git` command; a prompt-injected repo file can steer it. `rg` finds only traversal skip lists, no write-side guard.
- Fix: Add a shared `WorkspaceProtectedPaths` check (segment-based, case-insensitive on macOS/Windows after the same Unicode/trailing-dot normalisation as `WorkspaceRelativePath`) rejecting create/modify/rename/delete whose first segment is `.git` (also as a file/worktree pointer) or that is under `.arcanum/`, enforced in `WorkspaceFileFingerprintService.Resolve`, `SandboxedFileIo` writes and `PhysicalFileSystemWriter`, with an explicit operator opt-out. Hazard: the host legitimately writes `.arcanum` (`CampaignEndpoints.cs:162,481`), so the guard must apply only to model/API-driven file writes.
- Failing test: `ApplyPatchToolTests.Create_under_dot_git_hooks_is_rejected_and_nothing_is_written` (new `protected_path` error, file absent) and `SandboxedFileIoTests.Write_under_dot_git_is_rejected`, `PhysicalFileSystemWriterTests.WriteFileAsync_rejects_dot_git_paths`.
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` §11.6 and the `apply_patch` description (~1678); `docs/Arcanum.API.md` workspace file write errors
- Design conflict: none

### R-015 · S08-05 · Infra/Workspaces · `workspace_check` restore seeding walks the entire workspace four times per run, descending into `.git`/`node_modules`/`bin`/`obj`, and fails the run if any file there is unreadable
- Verification: Confirmed — V2 (mitigating: macOS-only, opt-in, jail-gated)
- Category: performance
- Location: `Infra/Workspaces/CodingTools/WorkspaceCheckRestoreArtifactSeeder.cs:86-95,156-172,332-338,354,1243-1256,1576-1590`; `Infra/Workspaces/CodingTools/WorkspaceCheckRuntime.cs:346-351,405-411,467-473`
- Defect: `Directory.EnumerateFiles(workspace, "*", Recurse)` yields every file under ignored trees and only then discards them (`HasIgnoredDirectory` post-filter), four times per call (SeedAsync, its internal `RevalidateManifest`, the runtime revalidation, the pre-start revalidation) with no wall-clock bound. `IgnoreInaccessible = false` makes one permission-denied directory inside an ignored tree throw `UnauthorizedAccessException`, mapped to `restore_required` ("run restore") for an unrelated reason; a real project under a directory literally named `bin`/`obj` is silently excluded.
- Fix: Replace the recursive enumeration with a manual BFS or `FileSystemEnumerable` with `ShouldRecursePredicate` pruning `.git`/`bin`/`obj`/`node_modules` before descending; enumerate once and hand the project list to the revalidation passes (keep one cheap pruned re-enumeration for the pre-start check so "input changed" still holds); treat `UnauthorizedAccessException` per pruned directory, not per run.
- Failing test: `WorkspaceCheckRestoreArtifactSeederTests.SeedAsync_does_not_descend_into_ignored_directories` (`node_modules/locked` chmod 000 on Unix; assert `Success`, no `restore_required`, no entry under `node_modules`/`.git` visited).
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` workspace_check restore-seed paragraph (state the pruning rule)
- Design conflict: none

### R-016 · S09-02 · Infra/InstallationReset · Resume arm omits the stopped-host issuer, so a resumed Workspace reset applies through a hard-wired Unavailable data service and is retired as a failure
- Verification: Confirmed — V3
- Category: reliability
- Location: `Infra/InstallationReset/InstallationResetService.cs:1248-1253,1291,1465-1469,1576,1584-1587,1641-1649,1675-1707` (retire at :1701); `InstallationResetExistingGrimoire.cs:109-117,877`; DI `ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:493-495`; `Cli/Commands/InstallationFactoryResetCommand.cs:270`; `InstallationResetApplyBoundary.cs:296-352,512`
- Defect: a Workspace record resumed in `Prepared` with `DataPlanIds.Length > 0` and no online completion calls `dataService.ApplyAsync`, which always returns `InventoryUnavailable` (the only registered `IInstallationResetDataService`); the fresh arm passes `stoppedHostIssuer`, the resume arm does not. `InventoryUnavailable` is not in the keep-alive list and the Workspace arm claims no nested receipt, so `writer.RetireAsync` runs and returns `Failure` although `PointOfNoReturn` is already true; the stopped-host path holding `RecoverWorkspaceResetAsync`/`RecoverFactoryResetAsync` is never reached. Bound: operator can re-run a fresh Workspace reset.
- Fix: Pass `stoppedHostIssuer: issuer` at `:1465`; better, make it a required parameter and delete the non-stopped-host `dataService.ApplyAsync` branch (a dead production path). Do not retire when `PointOfNoReturn` is already true unless the error is a proven pre-effect refusal. Verify `ContinueFullAsync` (`:1009`) only reaches `:1624` with an online completion before relying on it.
- Failing test: `InstallationResetServiceTests.Workspace_resume_at_prepared_point_of_no_return_applies_through_the_stopped_host_authority` (fake data service's `ApplyAsync` returns `InventoryUnavailable`, `ApplyUnderStoppedHostAuthorityAsync` succeeds; assert completion and `ApplyAsync` never called; today `Retired == true` and failure).
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` §10.20 reset-resume description
- Design conflict: none

### R-017 · S09-04 · Infra/InstallationReset · Service cancellation tests cannot detect the bug class they are named for
- Verification: Confirmed — V3 (nuance: deleting the whole resume-arm catch IS caught; the token-substitution mutation is the one that survives)
- Category: test-honesty
- Location: `tests/InstallationReset/InstallationResetServiceTests.cs:1288-1327,1330-1360,3151-3190,3942-3963,3985-4000`; production `Infra/InstallationReset/InstallationResetService.cs:1255-1277,1266-1268,1472-1494,1483-1485`
- Defect: both tests make a fake throw `new OperationCanceledException()` while the call passes `CancellationToken.None`; the fake `WriteAsync`/`WriteAuthenticatedAsync` never call `ThrowIfCancellationRequested` (the real `AdvanceAsync` does). Replacing `CancellationToken.None` with `cancellationToken` at `:1268`/`:1485` (the recurring "refused on the token that just failed" regression) leaves both green; `ApplyFreshUnderTestLockAsync` hard-codes `None` and the fresh arm has no cancellation test, so deleting its catch at `:1255` is also uncaught.
- Fix: Drive cancellation with a real `CancellationTokenSource` cancelled from inside the fake (`FakePreDataMutation.ExecuteAsync`/`FakeDataService.ApplyAsync`), pass `cts.Token`, make the fake `WriteAsync` call `cancellationToken.ThrowIfCancellationRequested()`, and add a token parameter to `ApplyFreshUnderTestLockAsync`.
- Failing test: `InstallationResetServiceTests.Fresh_apply_cancelled_after_point_of_no_return_checkpoints_with_an_uncancelled_token` (red against the `cancellationToken` mutation at `:1268`), plus the same hardening on the two existing tests for `:1485`.
- Docs: none
- Design conflict: none

### R-018 · S31-03 · Infra/InstallationReset · A kill during active-record publication leaves a `.tmp.<guid>` sibling that fails every later read closed, with no cleanup path
- Verification: Confirmed — V15 (trigger rare; the deliberate fail-closed inspection is documented, the missing piece is cleanup)
- Category: reliability
- Location: `Infra/InstallationReset/InstallationResetActiveAnchorStore.cs:397,410-441,478-487,576-620,695-698`; `InstallationResetActiveStore.cs:1076-1082`; `InstallationStartupProbe.cs`; tests `InstallationStartupProbeTests.cs:276-299`, `InstallationResetActiveStoreTests.cs:1411,1455,1486,1545`
- Defect: `ReplaceDurablyAsync` cleans its temp only in `finally`, which does not run on kill; nothing enumerates stale `*.tmp.*` siblings, and `InspectDirectory` treats any `<leaf>.tmp*` as `EvidenceFailure`/`ManualRecoveryRequired` on every `ProbePresence`/`ReadIfPresent`/`RequireNoEvidence`, before any secret read, so `serve` refuses to start until an operator deletes an unnamed file. The canonical file plus OS-credential anchor are the authority, so a `<leaf>.tmp.<32 hex>` can only be an unpublished copy. Every phase advance passes through this millisecond window.
- Fix: Under the held installation lock (recovery entry points only, never the read-only probes), delete only temporaries matching exactly `^<ActiveLeaf>\.tmp\.[0-9a-f]{32}$` (case-sensitive) via `IdentityOwnedFileSystemCleanup` (no-follow, identity-captured), then re-inspect; keep refusing every other `.tmp*` spelling and case-variant files; name the unrecoverable cases in the error text.
- Failing test: `InstallationResetActiveStoreTests.Recover_removes_an_orphaned_publication_temporary_and_resumes` (valid published record, plant `ActivePath + ".tmp." + Guid.NewGuid().ToString("N")` with partial bytes, `RecoverAsync(heldLock)`; assert success, record readable, temp gone); keep the non-conforming-name tests.
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` §10.20 (~759): state orphaned publication temporaries are removed during recovery
- Design conflict: none (DESIGN :759 "partial or substituted evidence remains blocked" is satisfied by the narrow exact-name removal)

### R-019 · S10-01 · Infra/Mcp · The in-process internal tool server is constructed with `logger: null` in production, so every `_logger?.Log*` call is a no-op
- Verification: Confirmed — V4
- Category: reliability
- Location: `Infra/Mcp/ConnectionManager/McpConnectionManager.Lifecycle.cs:660-673`; `Infra/Mcp/InProcessMcpTransport.cs:175,188`; `Infra/Mcp/ArcanumInternalToolServer.cs:43,215-218,257,330`; `Infra/Mcp/ConnectionManager/McpConnectionManager.TransportFactory.cs:152,178`; `Infra/Mcp/SdkMcpClientWrapper.cs:78,113,140`; DI `ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:~1631`
- Defect: the only production `CreateServerChannelPair` caller passes `logger: null`; ~54 `_logger?.Log...` sites (ExecuteCommand 13, CommunicationTools 13, Lexicon 6, FileTools 6, core 5, Covenant 4, Attachment 4, Saga 2, Helpers 1) never emit, while six tool-error strings tell the model and operator "See server logs" (`SandboxedFileIo.cs:152,252,481,490`, FileTools) and `execute_command` start failures are logged only there. The SDK client wrapper also gets no `ILoggerFactory` (`loggerFactory: null`).
- Fix: Add `ILoggerFactory` to `McpConnectionManager` and pass `CreateLogger<ArcanumInternalToolServer>()` at `Lifecycle.cs:673`; make the parameter non-optional on `CreateServerChannelPair`. Hazard (V4): do NOT pass a logger factory to the McpHttp `HttpClientTransport`/`SdkMcpClientWrapper` — the `McpHttp` client uses `.RemoveAllLoggers()` (`ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:~1612-1618`) because hosted MCP endpoints embed bearer tokens in the URL path and SDK logs could route the endpoint URI into the rolling log and `GET /api/logs`; wire only the internal-server logger unless the SDK's log content has been audited.
- Failing test: `McpConnectionManagerInternalServerLoggingTests.InternalServer_logs_tool_handler_failure_through_manager_logger` (capturing `ILoggerFactory`; trigger `LineHandlerFaultForTesting` or an IOException via `read_file_chunk`; assert an entry with category `ArcanumInternalToolServer`; red today).
- Docs: none
- Design conflict: none

### R-020 · S10-02 · Infra/Mcp · Workspace-local `mcp.json` servers can replace any built-in tool not on the six-name reserved list (`write_file`, `read_file_chunk`, `ask_human`, `scribe_lexicon`, ...)
- Verification: Narrowed (Important retained) — V4: requires the operator to have digest-approved that exact `mcp.json` via `POST /api/mcp/trust-workspace`; global servers cannot shadow (the internal row wins `TryAdd`); case variants do not replace (`indexByName` is Ordinal)
- Category: security
- Location: `Infra/Mcp/McpToolMerger.cs:17-33,82-132,141-239` (reserved check `:186`, override `:195-233`); `Infra/Mcp/ConnectionManager/McpConnectionManager.Merge.cs:19-135` (trust gate `:40-48`); `Infra/Mcp/InternalTools/ArcanumInternalToolServer.Registry.cs:28-76`
- Defect: `ReservedInternalToolNames` holds only `execute_command`, `apply_patch`, `workspace_check`, `search_workspace`, `propose_covenant`, `retire_covenant`. For any other internal name (`write_file`, `replace_text_block`, `read_file_chunk`, `list_directory`, `read_command_output`, `ask_human`, `scribe_lexicon`, `delete_lexicon`, `send_commlink_alert`, `petition_dungeon_master`, `adjust_initiative`, `cast_sending`, `dispatch_sending`, `continue_sending`, `search_archives`, `read_saga`, `attach_session_file`, `refresh_session_file`) an approved workspace-local server's same-named tool replaces the internal one with a bridge; the model, the name-keyed pipeline policy (`ToolRiskClassifier`, `NoForbiddenArts`, Sanctum path extraction) and the operator all believe it is the sandboxed built-in.
- Fix: In `MergeWorkspaceSurface` build the reserved set as the static list PLUS every `internalTagged` row name (ordinal and ignore-case) and apply it to local rows before the `TryGetValue` at `:195`; keep local-over-global override for external-vs-external names only. Hazard: the static list must stay because internal tools gated off for a session (`ask_human` non-streaming, `scribe_lexicon` with Lexicon off) are absent from `internalTagged`; a purely dynamic set would let an external server claim those names exactly when the built-in is unavailable.
- Failing test: `McpToolMergerTests.MergeWorkspaceSurface_workspace_local_server_cannot_replace_unreserved_internal_tool` (Theory over `write_file`, `read_file_chunk`, `ask_human`, `scribe_lexicon`, `replace_text_block`; `Assert.Same(internalRow.Tool, Assert.Single(merged))`; drop the `Write_File` case-variant row or assert coexistence).
- Docs: `docs/Arcanum.DESIGN.md` MCP tool merge/precedence (all internal names are unshadowable)
- Design conflict: none

### R-021 · S10-03 · Infra/Mcp · The "covenant tools are unshadowable" guarantee and the reserved-name set have no test

- Defect: both reservation Theories enumerate only `search_workspace`, `execute_command`, `workspace_check`, `apply_patch` (+ case variants); `rg -i covenant McpToolMergerTests.cs` is empty and the set is private. Deleting `CovenantToolNames.ProposeCovenant`/`RetireCovenant` from the set fails nothing, though the source comment calls it a security invariant.
- Fix: Add the two covenant names (and, after R-020, a Theory generated from `ArcanumInternalToolServer.RegisteredToolHandlerNamesForTests`) to both Theories.
- Failing test: `McpToolMergerTests.MergeWorkspaceSurface_external_servers_cannot_claim_covenant_tool_names` (`[InlineData("propose_covenant")] [InlineData("retire_covenant")]`; green today, red when the mutation is applied).

### R-022 · S10-04 · Infra/Mcp · A stdio/HTTP server that hangs during `initialize` surfaces as `OperationCanceledException`, aborting global init and the workspace tool surface and scheduling no restart backoff
- Verification: Confirmed — V4 (the wrapper's `CancelAfter` is armed before `SdkMcpClient.CreateAsync`, so its token always fires before the SDK's own timeout)
- Category: reliability
- Location: `Infra/Mcp/SdkMcpClientWrapper.cs:117-150`; `Infra/Mcp/ConnectionManager/McpConnectionManager.Lifecycle.cs:172-231,289-307`; `Infra/Mcp/ConnectionManager/McpConnectionManager.Partitions.cs:~208`; `Infra/Mcp/ConnectionManager/McpConnectionManager.Merge.cs:94`; `Infra/Mcp/McpConnectionManager.cs:300-311`; `Infra/Mcp/McpConfigModels.cs:22` (`AlwaysOn` defaults true)
- Defect: nothing converts the wrapper's own initialization timeout into a timeout error; `FinishStartAsync`'s `catch (OperationCanceledException)` disposes and rethrows, the entry is marked Error "start was canceled", and `ScheduleRestartBackoff` runs only on `startResult.IsFailure`, never on an exception. One wedged `npx` server therefore aborts the global-init loop (remaining servers never start) or `GetAvailableToolsAsync`, and the next request pays the 60 s timeout again every turn. No test covers handshake timeout.
- Fix: In `SdkMcpClientWrapper.InitializeAsync` catch `OperationCanceledException` when `initialization.IsCancellationRequested && !cancellationToken.IsCancellationRequested` and throw `TimeoutException`; `FinishStartAsync`'s generic catch then yields `Mcp.StartFailed` with backoff. `_initialized` stays false and the transport may be half-started, so rely on `DisposeClientAfterFailedStartAsync`.
- Failing test: `SdkMcpClientWrapperTests.InitializeAsync_throws_TimeoutException_when_server_never_answers_initialize` (in-memory transport never replies, 200 ms timeout, `Assert.ThrowsAsync<TimeoutException>`) plus a manager test that `entry.RestartAfterUtc` is set and the global-init loop continues to the next server.
- Docs: none
- Design conflict: none

### R-023 · S10-06 · Infra/Mcp · `Restart_increments_generation_and_invalidates_stale_handler` never touches production code
- Verification: Confirmed — V4
